Today, it is a hundred and two years since the Genocide of Pontian Hellenism, a black page in our history, which remains unjust to date. The Holocaust of our Pontian brothers was one of Turkey's most brutal crimes against humanity. An irreversible wave of brutality with prejudiced persecutions, torture, murder, coercive projects, disasters, plundering, displacement and an irreparable humanitarian disaster that counts the three hundred and three thousand lost souls. Irrespective of the victims who have either been sentenced to death, or who have been sentenced to live a terrible and horrific life. A sad addition to the long list of methodological extremism of the East, ending with crimes against Cypriots, Asia Minor and Armenians. To date, the official Turkish leadership has continued to stubbornly deny the Pontian Genocide and with impediment to it ranks it as the side-effects of a troubled era. On the other hand, we over the years have sometimes been lukewarm and sometimes strong, we have claimed, restored the historical truth and our victims. The occasional appeals in the international community have encountered a variety of reactions. On the positive side, we can credit the recognition of genocide from Armenia, Cyprus, Sweden, from nine states of America, from parts of Canada and from certain federal units in Russia, as well as from the Netherlands, Austria, South Australia and New South Wales. Unfortunately, the plenary session of our European Parliament is at the opposite side when the attempt to recognise the Genocide was spectacularly voted against. Of course, regardless of occasional reactions, we must never forget that history may be slow but does not forgive. We have seen this recently through developments in relations between the United States and Turkey. The start was made with the successive slaps of President Biden to the sultan, who started with the blockade of Turkey by the production of F-35 fighters and culminated in his decision to proceed with the official recognition of the Armenian genocide. In this context, which was obviously based on other selfish motives, we might be able to incorporate our own attempt to claim the recognition of Genocide and Pontian Hellenism. Of course, it is clear and self-evident that we cannot control the current balance of power in an equalisation of interests between the United States and Turkey. However, we can take advantage of developments by promoting our own fairness by drawing up a single and solid proposal for the recognition of Genocide and of the total losses of Hellenism at this stage. We are in a favourable position to make use of the restoration of historical truth, because if we do not fight it, no one will do it for us. Today more than ever we need unity towards a common legal demand. We need to keep our memory alive and our future firm, so we must move with honor and stubbornness in the fight for the justice of our ancestors no matter how difficult the outcome is, no matter how much we have to fight, because, as an old Pontou cognitive, the law of no matter what position or power is always to be restored. In those three hundred and fifty - three thousand Greek Pontus Christians were destroyed. Eight hundred and fifteen villages were excavated. Thousands of thirty - four churches were destroyed. Nine hundred sixty schools were demolished and four hundred thousand our outdated compatriots arrived in free Greece. They left behind a culture of about three thousand years old, a culture that blossomed for about eight centuries and offered mankind critical thought, research, politics and science. Ladies and gentlemen, at this point I would like to say that President Biden's decision to recognise the Armenian genocide is an exceptional fact for the following reasons: First of all, he puts the word 'genocide' into the world debate. Secondly, it indirectly recognises the genocide of all Christian populations in the east. Thirdly, it lights the green light and other governments to take similar steps. And fourthly, it gives us the opportunity to take advantage of the issue of the Genocide of the Greeks of the Pontou. President Biden took the first step. I'm sure others will follow. Because history is our identity, the history of the Greeks of the Pontus, the Thraces, the Greeks of Asia Minor is the same. Because the Genocide of the Greeks of Pontou is for all of us, it is for Hellenism, the basis for for forging our national conscience. Because the genocide of the Greeks of Pontou is not only a Greek issue, it is a global issue, such as the holocaust of the Jews by the Nazis, such as the Armenian genocide. It's a matter of humanity. ParlaMint-GR_2021-05-19-S1-commons.u10	I grew up listening, usually at night, to the low-speaking fiancées of my grandmas for the loved ones who were lost and slaughtered in front of their eyes. You understand that my relationship with the Pontian Genocide is experiential and because it may be that the thrill does not allow me to speak from the breasts I have decided to submit myself to the discipline of a text. Genocide is a crime against humanity for that, and it is unreserved. A genocide can occur twice, the first time as an event and the second as an oblivion of the drama. This risk was attempted by the Greek Parliament, when in 1994, on the initiative of PASOK MPs, recognised the Genocide of the Greeks of the Pontou and established as a day of remembrance on May 19th. In other words, it was not only the legitimate obligation of the state to recognize the blood river and the sacrifice of a community of Greeks, but also the solid national belief that within the silence of oblivion you are rooted out and thus vulnerable to ignorance the nation is unable to define the roads, which he deserves to walk towards the future. It is not easy to describe the barbarity suffered by those who have been lost and those who survived, the extermination of three hundred and fifty thousand souls with almost all the means that human radioactivity can devise, executions, inventions, endless marches of death, the work classes. It is not easy to describe the image of thousands of unscrupulous dead, the change in river flow from stowed bodies, hardships in snow or liquor. Let younger generations keep three images deep in their hearts and in their memory as a guide to their own national debt tomorrow. November 1916, in the town of Kerashunta many Romans are intimidated by persecution in their villages and cities, in the hope of greater protection. The Ottomans gather adults in the church of Agios Georgios, and whenever the number of gathered persons exceeds two hundred fifty persons, they are deported in an unknown direction. Infants and children are delivered to the nostalgic Chets, which carry them to the beach of Kerasounda and crush them among the rocks of the coast. December 1917, Austrian consul Kvitovski describes in a telegram to the Austrian Ministry of Foreign Affairs the advent in Samsonda of a Turkish captain, who is blurred by the massacre, urges the military commander of the region to stop the massacre, which the Turkish forces are provoking against the Pontian inhabitants of the wider Pafras region. The deep night of darkness never allowed us to know the fate of the Turkish captain. Image third, Santaya mother is forced to sacrifice her baby so that her crying does not betray the position of persecuted female children and old men hiding in the woods. An incredible trauma to the collective memory of the Pontius genus. Ladies and gentlemen, I do not have the requirement, nor do I maintain the illusion that a new-Ottoman could follow the shocking and, at the same time, teaching example of the gorgeous Villa Brad to apologise for the holocaust of the Jews. I have, however, a requirement for the Greek state to take the lead in the struggle offered by the Polish organisations around the world to be recognized by international organisations and foreign parliaments by the genocide of Pontian Hellenism, as happened with the Armenian genocide. I have the requirement that the Greek state be included on the agenda of its official diplomacy requests, arising from the tragedy of the Pontian Genocide, such as respect for and tradition of historical relics. I require the Greek state to have the historical truth that if the Jewish holocaust is condensed in Auschwitz's image, the Greek genocide of the Pond is condensed in the dramatic wording of the Polychrone Enecides "Auschwitz in flow". Remember his dense, historically accurate and painfully revealing description: "The genocide of a turk is a buffalo, wicked, oriental, has no theoretical background, but rather practical plaiting. The so-called deportations, exiles of the entire villages, those extermination routes in the snow of women's children and older people - men are already in the work battalions or in the army - naturally do not lead to any Auschwitz with evilly organized mechanisms of the natural extermination of people. Oh, no. But it was an Auschwitz in flow. People were dying on their way, they weren't walking to get somewhere, no, they were walking to die of hardships, coldness, hunger, human humiliation. That was the evil system, wickedly organized. There was no Auschwitz at the end, because for most of them there was no end. Their journey to death was death, not the end of the journey."

ParlaMint-GR_2021-05-19-S1-commons.u23	Thank you. Mr President, before I place myself on the bill, I too would like to refer to the day of today, the anniversary of the Greek genocide of the Pontou, a day of sad remembrance for all Hellenism that lives one of the most inhuman and appalling pages of history. In my place, even in Pieria, there are thousands of the Greeks of Pontus who settled after Genocide and their violent displacement and are today one of the most dynamic and creative parts of our local society. I, therefore, also honour today, with respect and excitement to the thousands of victims, the anniversary of the Renaissance of the Greeks of the Pontou, and strongly condemn such acts of appalling barbarism. I believe it is our duty to safeguard the memory of the victims so that such crimes against humanity will not be repeated in the future and to restore historical truth. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, the Government of Mr Mitsotakis is a government which tackles the problems face to face and gives solutions without calculating any political costs, let alone when the problem concerns the lives of thousands of children as in this case. Furthermore, the Minister for Justice, Mr Tsiaras, has repeatedly pointed out at committee meetings that the reform, which is introducing in relation to relations between parents and children, has been a matter which has remained for years in the Ministry's drawers. Since, in fact, this is a very sensitive social issue, the bill was formulated after having been placed in the public consultation for a long time, it was introduced for processing in the House without any pressure of time, and at the hearing of the bodies there was a double number - twenty-one overall body - to present their views. The great interest in society was confirmed by the many comments submitted in the public consultation of the bill. Fourteen thousand eight hundred twenty-one comments were posted on the relevant website, more than all those submitted in the last bills. The scale of the problem, ladies and gentlemen, we all know that. We all know that the judiciary and many times and police departments are shocked by the conflicts of parents, former couples, who are engaged in conflicts centred on their children. It was therefore not possible for that government and the Ministry to indulge in this major social issue. The same view was expressed by the representatives of operators, particularly the representatives of the legal world, who agreed on the need for the reform, because they themselves stated that a legal practice has been created which does not comply with the current conditions. That is what the rapid increase in family differences in recent years is witnessing in the courts. Indicatively, of the nineteen thousand ninety-nine cases brought to trial in 2016, we reached only four years in 2019 in the fifteen thousand eight hundred sixty-three cases. This reality is today regulated by the Ministry of Justice with a bill of a clearly pediatric section, establishing the almost self-evident, that is, the joint exercise of parental care by both parents in case of termination of the cohabitation, divorce, annulment of the marriage or termination of the cohabitation agreement. Having always been at the core of the child's interest, the bill introduces five innovative reforms to which I shall briefly refer. First reform: the joint and equally parental care of both parents is introduced for the first time in the event of separation. Parental care shall be the duty and right of the parents and shall include the custody of the child the management of his or her property and his or her representation in any case or act or trial concerning his or her child or property. So for all these critical issues about their child’s life and upbringing, both parents now have reason. From no point of view of this provision is the introduction of compulsory public custody, as several members of the opposition have mentioned. The next provision clearly provides for the possibility for parents to derogate from the rule of co-responsibility for parental care and to regulate differently, otherwise, the specific issues relating to parental responsibility. If they disagree, they shall first appeal to mediation and then to the court. There is therefore no horizontal mandatory application of the rule of public custody, since derogations from it are expressly laid down either by consensus or by decision of a court. Ladies and gentlemen, the reason for this fundamental change is the child's real interest. As we have heard from the institutions, all scientific studies have come to the assumption that children, who are growing up with the physical presence of both parents, are children with fewer problems, children with more balanced growth, who are able to cope more easily with the difficulties of life, become more easily integrated into society and achieve more. After all, a similar legislative framework in the exercise of parental care has already been applied successfully in all the Member States of the European Union other than Bulgaria, which is now moving in the same direction. In addition, particularly important innovation is the introduction of the statutory joint parental care by both parents and children who were born out of marriage, if they have been voluntarily or judicially recognised by the father. I note that it relates to quite a large number of children and effectively abolishes anachronistic discrimination in children in and out of marriage. Second reform is the introduction of the fighting presumption of one third of the child's minimum communication time with the parent with whom he does not live, so as to ensure increased communication and quality time for the child with both parents. As I have said several times, the presumption is combatable, so it is a country's return, and the judge may deviate from a third and determine less or more time of communication if they impose reasons on reasons relating to the conditions of life or the interests of the child and provided that his daily life is not disrupted, such as school, extra-school and social activities. Provision of the provision is particularly important as it ensures the stability of the child's life and, by extension, his mental equilibrium. And in any case this is a very balanced arrangement based on reason and always with compass the interests of the child. Besides, I think we all agree that ensuring a minimum of communication with both parents is very important first of all for the child itself for his personality and for his psychosomatic health. But for parents too, it can be a basis for dialogue and consultation in order to limit as little as possible tensions and litigation on communication issues. The third reform of the bill is to promote alternative methods of resolving family differences, including mediation. Parents will be able to regulate the specific issues of parental care for custody and communication first by written agreement between them. If they do not reach an agreement, they will be addressed to a specialised Ombudsman. In fact, because it has been said that some parents may not be able to afford, I would refer you, ladies and gentlemen, to Law 4640/2019 of mediation, which also applies to this case and provides for the benefit of the financial patients. I believe that the institution of mediation will help if not all of the parents to manage their separation peacefully, to avoid ruthless judicial battles, conflicts and extremes, which hurt the souls of their children. This is also one of the main objectives of the bill, namely strengthening the culture of consensus and finding solutions in a peaceful way so that all cases are not reached in courtrooms. Fourth reform of the bill: For the first time, objective criteria for the misuse of parental care are laid down which, if the judge finds that he can even remove parental care or custody from the parent responsible and only entrust it to the other parent, or even to a third party, if it requires the interest of the minor child. The criteria, for example, include unjustified refusal to pay food, the responsible violation of the judgments and agreements of parents, the burglary of the relations of the child with the other parent, and the definitive condemnation of the parent for domestic violence or for crimes against sexual freedom or for crimes against the economic exploitation of sexual life. A number of objections and reservations have indeed been raised here. Do you, ladies and gentlemen, think that the Greek judge will not take into account all the parameters, if he decides? Will it not take into account all the facts of each case? I have complete confidence in the fair judgment of the Greek judge who will appreciate any real incidents in the formation of his judiciary. What the order simply does is provide the judge with a basis, some extra legal instruments, that is. Besides, the judiciary officials, who will avenge family differences - and that is how we get to the fifth and perhaps more important reform of the bill - will have attended specific seminars and educational programmes. The seminars will be conducted by experts who have dealt with the issue, such as specialist psychologists, sociologists and social workers, as well as teachers of higher education institutions and judiciary officials. The contents of the seminars will include international conventions, in particular the Istanbul Convention to prevent and combat violence against women and family violence. It is doing so - and it is certain that the results will be positive, and that the representatives of the bodies have also expressed - that the training of the judiciary in the new forecasts and social needs will only have positive results. What is noteworthy, ladies and gentlemen of counter-population, is that your contribution to the parliamentary dialogue was limited only to trying to degrade the bill. Your only proposal that was positive was that of establishing family courts. Your only proposal, which was positive, was that of establishing family courts. An excellent proposal, but at the moment, at least directly, is not objectively applicable. In any case, SYRIZA's colleagues on this matter should first make their own self-criticism. Not only did a family court set up for four and a half years, but they did not dare touch this sensitive social issue, which we dared to do today, nor even dared to train judges with special training seminars. This government, however, dares and takes an important step towards helping and contributing to the resolution of family differences and one of the very positive steps is the introduction of training seminars to the judiciary. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] Ladies and gentlemen, the dialogue that has taken place with the social actors, parties and society has lasted for more than a year and a half. Comments, objections, comments and proposals from various bodies throughout the public consultation process, and most of the proposals from the official bodies were accepted by the Minister, as did representatives of the bodies themselves. Indicatively, the final text of the bill incorporated major remarks from the Citizens' Counselor, the National Committee on Human Rights and the Association of Judges and Prosecutors, which I mentioned in detail in my previous position. So what one cannot dispute is that this draft law, as has finally been formulated, is with certainty the result of a fruitful and exhaustive dialogue, a product of the composition of the views of civil society, expressed in a record of participation in public consultation. In view of the above, ladies and gentlemen, it is a real pity that, as far as it seems, we have not finally managed to go along with this great reform, which, as I have already pointed out, has no party mark at all. What is trying - and I repeat - to do this bill is to lay a basis for dialogue and consultation between divorced parents. Because I believe - we all believe - that people who have shared a small part of their lives can neglect their selfishness and change a page based on the good of their children.

So when with mathematical formulas you try to allocate the child’s time between the two parents, on the substance even for the parent, who does not have the child’s custody, you are replacing the child’s ability to make a substantial contribution to raising his child from finally obtaining a sterile communication time. The common exercise of parental care can take many forms, taking into account various factors, such as the age stage of development of the child, the stage of education, its pre-existing mental state, and the conditions of life and work of its parents. This is understandable that it cannot be achieved with this typical balance of the time of the child, where it actually focuses exclusively on the bill. In this way, the responsibility of parents is eventually fragmented. ParlaMint-GR_2021-05-19-S1-commons.u37	Thank you very much, Mr President. Mr President, Mr President-in-Office, ladies and gentlemen, according to Article 21 of our Constitution, the family as the foundation of the maintenance and promotion of the nation, as well as marriage, maternity and childhood, are under the protection of our state. The state, therefore, takes care to ensure decent living conditions for all citizens by means of a minimum guaranteed income system, as laid down in the law. Secondly, the multitude families, the disabled of war and peaceful period, the victims of war, the widows and orphans who have fallen into war, and those suffering from incurable physical and spiritual or mental illness have the right to special care by the state. Thirdly, the State takes care of the health of its citizens and takes special measures to protect the youth, old age, disability and the care of the most deprived. Also, fourthly, the acquisition of residence by those who are deprived of it or who are inadequately resident is the subject of special care by our state and, fifthly, the design and implementation of demographic policy and the taking of all the necessary measures are also an obligation of the state. According to Article 2(1) of our Constitution, respect and protection of human value are the primary obligation of our state. In addition, Article 5(1) of the Constitution stipulates that, first, everyone has the right to freely develop his or her personality and to participate in the country's social and economic life, in so far as he does not infringe the rights of others and does not violate the Constitution or good customs. In addition, Article 25(1)(c) of our Constitution stipulates that these rights also apply to relations between individuals - and here is the important detail - to which they belong. This is the most famous of you, more than I imagine, the principle of three-energy. Finally, Article 28(1) of our Constitution stipulates that the generally acknowledged rules of international law, as well as all international conventions since their ratification by law and their entry into force, according to the terms of each of them, form an integral part of the domestic Greek law and even prevail from any other provision contrary to them. These constitutional provisions, you know, Mr President, Minister, ladies and gentlemen, are not just guidelines, in our opinion, but are imperative rules of our law. In other words, the right to the protection of childhood is also an institution guarantee. The joint legislator is therefore required to lay down regulations which are appropriate and necessary for the protection of childhood. However, with sadness, Mr President, once again we realise that provisions introduced by this bill are completely in breach of the constitutionally guaranteed rights and, indeed, of younger people, of our young people, who need special care and attention, without prejudice to their real interests for the sole purpose of serving specific 'client' interests. In particular, in the second paragraph of Article 5 of this draft law, which we are discussing today, I quote: "In the best interests of the child, served..." - according to the reasoning of your draft law, Minister - "in particular from the genuine involvement of both parents in raising and taking care of him, as well as from preventing his or her relationships with each one of them, the decision of the court should be taken when deciding on the award of parental care or on the way in which he is exercised." In other words, the court should also look forward to this. And it continues: “The court ruling takes into account parameters... - you say this, that is, your law - "... such as the ability and intention of each of the parents to respect the rights of the other, the behaviour of each parent during the previous period and its compliance with his or her legal obligations, judicial decisions, appeals and prior agreements concluded with the other parent concerning the child". In the present case, the legislature disregarding the true interest of the child, as defined in Articles 21(1), 2(1) and 5(1), as well as Article 25 of our Constitution, as well as the international conventions that our country has ratified and applied, in accordance with Article 28 of the Constitution and which are no more than a free and decent living and form of personality, considers, as a pretext in the interests of the child, the effective involvement of both parents in its upbringing and care, as well as in preventing relations with each of them by establishing criteria which are neither relevant to the interests of the child, but are in fact trying to protect the interests of its adult parents. In this way, however, both the international treaties relating to the protection of children and the provisions of the abovementioned articles of the Constitution are violated. ParlaMint-GR_2021-05-19-S1-commons.u61	Thank you, Mr President. The debate is of interest and political and ideological interest, it is gaining purely legal status. According to Article 21, childhood is under the protection of the State. The interest of the child as a vague legal concept must be sought by the judge in a particular case and in relation to any particular child. Therefore, restrictions that are put to the law by suggesting to the meaning of the child’s interest some elements against others, such as the non-disorder of his relationship with parents, may be at the expense of specific children. And here, in fact, the state's constitutional obligation is being violated. As an vague legal concept, the interest of the child is specified in each particular case by evaluation criteria, which are drawn, inter alia, from the findings of psychology. The Court is therefore allowed to give it different content in a particular case in the light of the specific nature of any legal relationship and its subjects. The personalised crisis is admitted to constitute also the application of the requirement of Article 2(1) of the Constitution, which excludes stereotypes from the assessment of individuals and personal relations. Therefore, from the point of view of the case, paragraph 2 also requires a wide range of intervention to be given to the judge on a case-by-case basis and under the specific circumstances. Therefore, with the structure prevailing in the contested provision, we think that the issue of unconstitutionality really arises. I should also like to remind you that we have taken note of the report by the Scientific Committee of Parliament and the provision of Article 7, which says that it is an unequal treatment for the person liable to inform a parent who may, under conditions, be inconsistent with the purpose of Article 4 of the Constitution. So we are also told by the Scientific Committee of Parliament that there is another lack of constitutional content. So I put this in the Members' judgement too. Finally, the compulsory communication of the other parent in at least 1/3 can be at the expense of the child, which may be called upon to coexist with a parent operating negatively, maliciously or violently in relation to that parent. So, in this case, we also have a constitutional problem precisely because the margins, set beforehand by the legislator to the judge, effectively limiting his judiciary crisis, are too narrow. For all these reasons, we are voting in favour of the anti-constitutional proposal.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-05-19-S1-commons.u81	Ladies and gentlemen, I will start with one comment. Ladies and gentlemen, the draft law under discussion, which is carefully and mildly, attempts, after some forty years, to introduce small scales, but necessary reforms in the most sensitive area of family law, relationships between parents and children. It follows from the basic finding that the current regulations reproduce a stereotype created by the consolidation of the case-law of our courts, which, in the event of a spouse's dimension, confer, in the vast majority of cases, parental care with stereotypes and typical reasons. It lays the draft law as the foundation for the equal participation of parents in the upbringing, physical, mental, emotional and spiritual development of their children. This is happening - and the law says so - during the wedding. It is also desirable that after the marriage’s dimension or solution there should be an equal participation of parents. That is why the draft law we are discussing gives priority to parents, who knowing better than anyone else their child and his interests must agree on the issues of custody of the person, of residence, of nutrition and of communication with the parent with whom he will not reside together. On the issue of custody, for example, parents can freely agree either the diligence or the sole custody of one of them. In the event of disagreement, they shall be given the opportunity to appeal to mediation. If they do not want mediation or if mediation fails then, as is the case in any favoured society, the judge has the final word. The legal evidence, however, that the law stipulates that the judge must take into account different quantitatively, but also qualitatively from the existing regulations, which is not bad, on the contrary, is good because he gives the judge greater power to form his judiciary and extends, in the context of the system of inquiry, his or her field of inquiry. A fundamental rule, which runs all the parental and child arrangements, is that parents share the parental care of their child. This rule follows from the basic scientific assumption that the proper development of the child presupposes the active presence of both parents until adulthood. The "equivalent", for which much has been done, does not serve "awkward purposes", but seeks emphatically to mark the equal participation of parents in the care of their children. The allegations, which have been heard, that, in this way, the compulsory alternating residence for the child is, in essence, simply the pretexts of those who want to vote against the bill. On the substance, ladies and gentlemen, these arguments have no basis, because the issues referred to by the spokesmen of the Opposition are specifically and exhaustively dealt with in Articles 3, 8 and 12 of the draft law and, if one reads the bill, it cannot be thought indirectly or directly of the regulation of the bill, which is, on the contrary, other provisions of the 'import' - conscientiousness and compulsory alternating residence. This is, in my view, legal paradoxes. An important provision is also the introduction of a battle presumption of one third for the communication of the child with the parent with whom he does not live. This presumption is not arbitrary, but you should be aware - I think it was said at the meetings in the committee concerned - that there are findings of modern studies which determine the minimum amount of time for the parent to communicate with his or her child to about 35%. Nearly all the speakers referred to the importance of communication between the child and the parent who is not living in order to strengthen the mental bond and the role of the parent in the child's psychosocial development. However, when the time comes for this to come, there are several justifications, such as that it is not, in the first instance, deliberately and legally correct to introduce a quantitative criterion to the regulation of personal relations and that the reference period for the provision is not specified, so as to refuse, in the end, the vote in favour of Article 13 of the draft law, which provides for one-third of the total time, but - and this is particularly important - the basic condition laid down in this provision, namely that 1/3 is provided, provided that the daily life of the child is not disturbed. And this condition, who judges her? Sire, it's the parents who judge her. But if parents disagree, then who is the final judge on whether this condition is met? He's the judge. [[At this point, the Second Vice-President of Parliament, Mr HARALABOS ATHANAOU, occupyes the President-in-Office of the Council.]] It also lays down objective criteria for the misuse of parental care, which are necessarily taken into account by the court, in the regulation of matters of parental responsibility and care for children such as non-payment of maintenance, non-respect for judicial decisions and agreements between divorced parents and the burglary of relations between the child and the other parent. That is why there is a minimum presumption of contact time with the parent with whom he does not live under the same roof. The introduction of this presumption, which is estimated at one third of the total year, creates an existing basis for dialogue between parents in order to avoid tensions between them on the right to communication. Exclusion or restriction of such communication is certainly possible, but only for extremely serious reasons. The Law of the Ministry of Justice also establishes objective criteria for the misuse of parental care. Among those criteria, taken into account by the court in dealing with matters of parental responsibility and custody of children, include non-payment of nutrition, non-respect for the judgments and agreements of divorced parents and the erosion of relations between the child and the other parent. Thus family law proceeds from the one-dimensional exercise of parental care as a rule by both parents. And I say as a rule, because parents are always given the opportunity to agree something else. ParlaMint-GR_2021-05-19-S1-commons.u103	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, any reform of family law must be a process in which society as a whole is involved, because it is concerned with society as a whole. The draft law seeks to establish compulsory conscientiousness and alternating residence, without, of course, making explicit reference to them. Consistency is already regulated by current law and when there is a weakness of parental agreement, the court specialises in the way it is exercised, first defending the best interests of the child. In any change in the relationship between parents and children, the basic criterion and ultimately a request must be the interest of the child, the fulfilment of his or her needs and the protection of his or her rights. This serves the interest of the child protected by the Constitution and international conventions. Because the child is an arbitrary person, who has the same rights, and these rights must not involve the rights and wishes of parents, much more when there is a stop to married cohabitation. It should therefore be the approach on a case-by-case basis and in the light of the particular circumstances of each family. The court is therefore given the opportunity to choose the most appropriate arrangement of parental care and care, as dictated by European law incorporated into Greek legislation for the protection of the child, as well as by various provisions of the International Convention on the Rights of the Child. The bill requires that - jointly and equally - the exercise of parental care in an automatic manner. The reference to the exercise of parental responsibility is, in our view, inappropriate, inaccurate and dangerous, since it is not even provided for in marriage. It is a sign of imposing compulsory condolence, as the report of the House's Scientific Service says. The 'equivalent' may be replaced by 'equivalent' so that it cannot be misinterpreted, since the rate, which has a qualitative and non-quantitative dimension, is included in the concept of joint exercise. In accordance with the proposed provisions, parental care may be removed from the parent if it is deemed to be responsible for disrupting the emotional relationship between the child and the other parent and his family, and for the challenge of breaking relations with them in any way. In this way vague and vague criteria are introduced, which may even lead to the removal of parental care, while in contrast to that, it is predicted that a abused parent can exercise parental care or come into contact with the child until it is finalised, which of course requires time, because it is known that the relevant procedure is timely. This is contrary to common sense and to the Istanbul Convention and, of course, to the interests of the child, which requires that any suspicion of abuse should be investigated by means of criminal prosecution in order to remove the rights of the suspect. In this context, forced condolence, even in cases under investigation, puts the child at risk and the most vulnerable parent. The solution, therefore, is not forced conscientiousness, nor, of course, the distribution of time in a compulsory manner and without individualisation. With regard to the minimum communication time, the forecast one/3 of the total year is an indefinite arithmetic, quantitative size that refers to assets and property with the participation in acquisitions and which is not offered to regulate communication issues for parents and children. In addition, time-calculating problems arise, taking into account the fact that working relationships now, in this case and parents, are not characterised by stability. Apart from setting up family courts, there is also a need for the creation of supportive social structures, which are provided with scientists, family social workers, paedophiles, and child psychologists, who will support families by collaborating in the work of custody. Instead, the bill introduces exceptional training programmes for judges and prosecutors who will judge family affairs. The child is treated as a passive recipient of parental rights and not as a real provider of needs that require satisfaction. ParlaMint-GR_2021-05-19-S1-commons.u139	Ladies and gentlemen, after a long-term public debate, with proposals, conflicts and contradictions, the Ministry of Justice tabled today's draft law on the reform of family law. Its purpose, as described in Article 1, is to serve the best interests of the child. The crucial question, then, is: Is the purpose served? Is the child's interest strengthened? Is he protected by the new bill? Unfortunately, the answer is easy. Oh, no. Ladies and gentlemen, in fact, the bill emphasises the importance of continuing, joint parental care after parental separation. We all agree on this in principle, as long as the guide remains emphatically the interest of the child and not the interest of the parents. Because this bill has been written as being directed to a parent, the one with whom the child does not live, even when it does not regulate matters relating to him directly. And the question again is: is the child-centered nature of family law altered? Yeah, he's devastated. In particular, the obligation to provide the other parent with prior information on simple, daily or urgent issues is introduced when the Civil Code gives parents the right to attempt on their own in these matters. In other words, the new provision, without abolishing that right, deactivates it, subject to the approval of the other parent. In addition to the legal failure, this provision is practically impossible. It will increase parental indebtedness. In the case of their disagreement, the daily life of the child itself will be at a dead end. Even more serious problems arise the provision, which provides that parents continue to take parental care together and equally. We have heard the Minister himself say that the introduction of the term 'equivalent' is indicative of the desire for common parental care after the separation. In any case, a legal text is not a wish. Moreover, this desire is fully covered by the word'remaining in the common of parental responsibility'. In addition, according to the general provision of Article 1510 of the Civil Code, parents do not exercise parental care either during the marriage. After all, the issues of parental equality have been resolved by the 1983 law, where the constitutional principle of gender equality has been fully reflected in family law. The worst thing, however, is that here the term "equal" opens the way of horizontal treatment for all children. A child, that is, will be treated equally with a 15 - year - old child. The UN Convention on the Rights of the Child expressly states that the interests of the child are not intact and specialises in any particular case. It was not taken into consideration apparently. The legislator himself provides for and protects the interests of the child by introducing it as a vague legal concept and as such can only be specialized by the natural judge. The legislator cannot replace the judge. The term'removes', now, in the provision providing for the time of communication, wrongly confers the right to communicate with a parent who does not reside with the child in the nature of the obligation. Communication is a right protected by law. It can't be forced to do anything. Again, the Minister explained that these conditions are indicative and that the judge is obviously free to decide what is best for the child and on this matter. This is how one wonders: is it a reform of a bill, which is set up on indicative terms and regulations? The answer is unequivocal. No, it's not reform. Evidently those terms, 'equivalent' and'removed', are laid down in order to bind the Court's reasoning and judgment. In fact, they lead to an interpretation of a regular exercise of parental care with compulsory alternating residence. It is therefore necessary to clarify whether or not the two conditions should be removed. To provide for the definitive conviction of abuse offences as a case of misuse of parental care in practice, this means that the child will remain exposed to parental care for years. ParlaMint-GR_2021-05-19-S1-commons.u151	Thank you, Mr President. With this draft law on parental care, the New Democracy Government is trying to give the impression to the Greek society that this is a brave reform aimed at serving the best interests of the child. Indeed, the Minister, Mr Tsiaras in an interview, said that the draft law is very close to the legislation of other European countries. But let's see what's really going on. In Europe, as at international level, there is a variety of forms for the distribution of parental care and custody. There are even differences in the content of these concepts. So what is this approach to other countries? In many countries common parental care or care for parents is laid down, in particular by mutual agreement, with this trend to be extended. Yes, but neither the principle of equal time for both parents, as is the case in this draft law, nor the principle of alternating residence after the dimension, which was ultimately not included in the draft law as submitted. Also, even in those countries where the joint exercise of parental or diligent care, whether compulsory or not, is very small, the percentage of divorced couples who choose this type of custody exercise. Studies suggest that the proportion of children who have been divorced jointly is about 30% in regions of the United States, 25% in Norway and less than 20% in the United Kingdom. In many capitalist states the parental joint care is provided for by mutual agreement between parents themselves. Regardless of whether they have introduced conscientiousness or not, the reason why more active role is now required by the father is not only the necessary support, communication that must have with the child, but also the fact that a greater percentage of mothers in relation to the past work, with what this implies for their absence from home and child rearing. That is why the trend in the majority of the Member States of the European Union and in the United States is to extend the network of services to care for children, the elderly, but not under the responsibility of the State, with structures, social benefits and by saying, but largely traded and privatised. This also includes family advice and mediation, extremely expensive and time-consuming. That is why we have also tabled the amendment on the discharge of these provisions. In Germany, in the event of divorce, the parents maintain common parental responsibility in the event that none of them have applied for sole custody in the competent court. The German Civil Code does not exclude joint custody, and it is expressly permitted for the courts to decide on compulsory conscientiousness without the prior consent of both parents. There is compulsory joint custody in France, Belgium, Sweden, Denmark and in certain states of the United States of America. Even in these cases of compulsory conscientiousness, it does not have the same content everywhere, and there may be - and there may be - modifications to the law and to the case law itself. In Sweden, for example, the principle of common legal and not physical custody of children is applied. In other words, the two parents co-decide on a number of critical issues relating to the development of the child, they distribute what is involved in his custody. California, which was the first state to establish compulsory conscientiousness, equal to the year to both parents and alternating residence in 1969, amended the law in a direction only if both parents have jointly requested it and the interests of the child are maintained. France, Belgium, Denmark, Sweden, Switzerland, Germany, Austria, Catalonia, Australia, Mexico, Brazil and in seventeen States of the United States of America, the alternating residence is also applicable. Finally, international experience itself teaches us that changes in legislation on child care do not automatically result in changes in everyday life and relationships between parents and children. It is confirmed that such complex issues are not simply dealt with by laws and judicial decisions. That is why the percentage of divorced couples, who choose this type of custody, is very small even in the countries that are compulsory. For example in France, although there is compulsory diligence in law and changing residence, in 73% of divorce cases, mother exercises her own custody. French fathers participate three times less than mothers in childcare. The above suggests that conspiracy either the equal presence or the alternating residence is not applied and cannot be applied in a horizontal and mechanistic manner everywhere. ParlaMint-GR_2021-05-19-S1-commons.u179	Ladies and gentlemen, the subject under regulation is a field of flammable social relations. One can often meet, instead of logic, personal conflict and passion. That is why we have encountered conflicts between each other in the public debate, not only in the House today or in the parliamentary committee, but also in the scientific dialogue and between bodies. That was why, Minister, at the last meeting of the parliamentary committee, I suggested that you look for converging areas and meet, before the start of the plenary, with the rapporteurs of the parties, in order to find commonly accepted solutions, which I believe we can even now find. Joint parental care is absolutely necessary and desirable. I don't think anyone can argue with that. But, when marriage is solved, we need to find those operating rules so that the exercise of common parental responsibility is pragmatic and beneficial for the child. Otherwise, we may be confronted with insurmountable problems of everyday life. I will make a few suggestions, Minister, making my role a synthetic one and I hope you can hear us. I begin with the comments of the Scientific Committee of the Parliament. I suppose you have rightly diagnosed that the Scientific Committee of the Parliament in its report is not flattering on the arrangements you are proposing. I would say that it is rather convicted and I would suggest that you adopt other ideas, as you have just announced two of them, and the improvements you are proposing are positive. And I'm explaining. The first article of the bill defines as an interpretative rule the provisions of the bill in particular the Istanbul Convention and other international conventions. You are right to accept the point made by the Scientific Service of Parliament that, among the conventions which must be mentioned and which constitute an interpretative rule for the provisions you are proposing, it must also be the International Convention on the Rights of the Child. Because it is strange that the effort of the government and the Convention on the Rights of the Child should be defined as a child's rights as being an interpretative, guiding rule. So it's right that the integration you've accepted is correct. Secondly, the Scientific Service also told you that since we choose to refer to the Istanbul Convention, we should also respect it absolutely. Up to now mediation, which is a solution, a possibility for the philosopher's couple, could be about the full extent of the quarrel and even domestic violence. However, the Istanbul Convention explicitly prohibits mediation in the event of domestic violence. Therefore, we should exclude mediation in such cases, and therefore the correction is correct. However, other improvements must also be made. You say that parental care, the joint exercise of parental care, when the marriage is solved, must serve the best interests of the child. The expression ‘best interest’ is unscientific and does not meet either in the texts of the international conventions or in the applicable Civil Code or in the case-law of the supreme courts of the country. 'Best interest' means absolutely nothing. It's in the best interests of the child. As Greek case law states, the interests of the child are the whole of their moral, intellectual and life-rights, which, with their respect, facilitates the creation of an integrated personality. So it adds nothing, rather confusion, the expression 'best interest'. I would ask you to withdraw, delete the word 'best'. It is enough that the Committee on Legal Affairs and Citizens' Rights suggests that the common exercise of parental responsibility should serve the interests of the child. Next observation. You say that parental care should be exercised, in the case of a marriage solution, jointly and equally. What does "also" mean? It does not help you to "equate" to anything and I will explain it. If the equally exercise of common parental responsibility has the meaning of 'equivalent', you do not need it, because it is already laid down as a rule in Article 5 of your draft law, in the third paragraph, the principle of equality, in which it is recorded precisely that the decision of the court must also respect equality between parents and not discriminate on grounds of sex, sexual orientation, race, language, religion or other matters. If you mean ‘equal’, then you should state it, enter it in your text. But that is wrong, because it will create many practical issues. That is why I propose that you adopt a much more delicate wording of the legislative committee. I read on behalf of my fellow Members the wording of the Committee on Legal Affairs and Citizens' Rights, which I consider to be more practicable: 'The parent with whom the child does not reside retain the right to more extensive personal communication as far as possible'. This wider personal communication condemns and 1/3 that you, as an imputable time, implied. It could be much more than 1/3 or less, but the possibility of wider personal communication is, however, a beautiful wording, much more complete and more coherent than you are proposing. This is a unilateral and irreversible contract between the two parties of the broken marriage. ParlaMint-GR_2021-05-19-S1-commons.u191	Madam President, Minister, Minister, ladies and gentlemen, it is clear that this is the first major reform of family law thirty-eight years after Law 1329/83. Ladies and gentlemen, I am in favour of equality between sexes, men and women and the equal treatment of their obligations and their rights in all appearances of life. I believe that the mother and father, equally and with the characteristics that nature itself has given them, must be forced, but also facilitated in the custody of children who have themselves brought them into the world, but also in both of them to take the responsibilities of this diligence. This is what the bill does, if we want to see it objectively and without focusing on hypotheses that we often do. How many then were working women? Was there a cohabitation agreement then? Is social information and information the same? Do the children and their upbringing have the same requirements? Ladies and gentlemen, the government could not deal with an issue that burns and yet, with a sense of duty and responsibility, dare and proceeds to the first great reform of family law, with the aim of improving the position of the child and with the aim of respecting gender equality, respect for women's rights and balanced intervention in society without extremism. This bill presents five innovations: Firstly, the joint and equal exercise of parental care after the separation of the two parents. Secondly, it integrates mediation as a precursor before divorced one ends up with the natural judge. Thirdly, it introduces the introduction of a battle criterion for 1/3 of the child's communication time with each parent, an innovation that will also help in the consultation before the judge. Fourthly, it lays down objective criteria for the judge to judge how parental care should be distributed. And fifthly, it provides for the training and adaptation of judges to this new philosophy on family law. And the fact is that the bill was widely publicized and consulted by long compiling most of the comments in consultation. Indeed, for many it was a specific field of controversy. Ladies and gentlemen, this bill is certainly not a field of political controversy, and much more it cannot be a field of combat for the two sexes or a vehicle to carry each one's experiences and social experiences to Parliament. The bill modernises family law and introduces the consensual custody of children, whose main objective is to protect them. I have followed and listened carefully to the positions of all the bodies at the hearing of the Committee on Public Administration and Justice. My feeling is that, despite their individual differences in some issues, the bill covers with its philosophy the majority of the bodies. Indeed, the bill responds to what the Greek society is asking for today, and indeed to the additions made by the Minister responsible, it is further clarified what was the point of criticism, since it is now clear that it does not necessarily provide for conscientiousness or, obviously, that it was a product of social pressure. On the contrary, it meets the recommendations of the Citizens' Counselor for changing legislation and different communication between children and parents, improves our country's position, which is called on every single one by the European Court of Human Rights for similar cases and improves the whole framework for defining the conditions for children's conscientiousness. The main axis of the present is the maintenance of the common presence and participation of both parents in the upbringing of their children and after the end of the marriage or cohabitation agreement. That is to say, the principle of equality of parental responsibility and rights towards the child is safeguarded by the ultimate benefit which, as I have said before, is no more than the child itself, which will continue to enjoy the care of both of its parents. And let them understand who or whoever considers their exclusive right to custody to be wrong. In this context, the legal residence of the child is clearly determined, while the acts which the parent decides on his own and the cases in which the two parents are required to decide jointly are laid down. In order to address the difficulties that may arise during the conspiracy of this draft law, this Legislation restores the consensual communication of parents into their legitimate obligation to reach mutually acceptable solutions either on their own or with the help of an intermediary where this is deemed necessary. In the event of deadlock, as has always been done, there is always recourse to a court, which will provide an appropriate solution on a case-by-case basis. Settlement of these cases, according to this bill will be carried out by judges, and that is, as I said, one of the innovations of the bill, who will have attended special training seminars at the National School of Judges by qualified staff, including international conventions, with the most basic of the Istanbul Convention. This regulation comes as a response to another concern which has been heard, in particular that of the catastrophe so-called Istanbul Convention, which, as has been stated in Article 1, is the basis on which the provisions of the present provisions will be interpreted and applied. In addition, Article 14 refers to the criteria for assessing the misconduct of parental duties, including violent and malicious behaviour, and sets out the similar consequences including partial or total removal of parental responsibility or conscientious or diligence. In any case, the judge may deviate from the now fighting presumption of 1/3, according to the overall attitude of the parent accused of violence, resulting in the protection of the other members of the family, as is the case so far. Finally, in the context of modernization of the existing legal framework, the arrangements for intangible divorce and the assimilation of off-marriage children with those in marriage are also included in the application of the provisions on parental care after divorce or annulment of marriage. Ladies and gentlemen, given that the model of the modern Greek family has changed, calling for the increasingly equal participation of both parents in child rearing, it is our duty to preserve the role of both of these parents as well as after the end of a marriage. And it is clear that this bill offers a new basis for dialogue for divorced parents and is an important tool for judges to make the best decision in the interests of children. Thank you.

Whether within or outside marriage, the modern woman has - and this must be guaranteed by the state - the right to work and social life and we are not allowed to confirm these stereotypes. Of course, it is a question of being in marriage, within a normal and loving partnership. But when this is not possible, the end of the world does not come. It's not over either Mother's life, father's life, and certainly not children's. We simply need to ensure a modern approach in the context of responsible understanding and civilized behaviour, so that both continue their social, family and working life always and above all, with the main concern of the interests and future of the children. That is why I would like to congratulate you, Minister, on the legislative initiative, which, in the context of gender equality, recognises the co-responsibility of the two parents in the care of their children, while ensuring that their differences and problems do not become their own problems. It is a matter of culture and I am sure that the young Greeks and the new Greek women are already facing this issue with enormous maturity. Ladies and gentlemen, the family is, in fact, the foundation of every culture and every human society, it is the place in which we are growing up and developing, the refuge in which our psychic, personality and characters are shaped, it is the thermospheric nuisance that is lurking us until the time we rely on our own supplies to deal with the world. It is a living organization that is evolving and reformed to meet the challenges of every age and age. Many have changed in recent years in the structure and functioning of the Greek family. The roles have changed and adapted to the requirements of modern life. The mother works and does not always stay at home, but the modern father also has an active involvement in the daily care of children and households. Together they try to find the new balance between work and family obligations, which is not always easy. Of course, it is ideal for there to be two parents working in harmony, united and loved under the same roof. But that seems to be becoming more and more difficult and reflected in numbers and statistics which we cannot simply ignore. In modern societies, as in our country, divorces, unfortunately, are growing, people are divided more easily, while in the middle of all of these are children. These children must be protected. We have to give these children the opportunity to always have both parents. It is therefore necessary, almost forty years since the last reform of our institutional framework, to adapt to keep pace with the new data and to deal with a different social reality. This bill wants to give all those tools, which will allow two people, who cannot or do not want to be together, to care in the best possible way for their children and especially to make the transition from cohabitation to separation as painless as possible. At the same time, it gives every parent the right to maintain his communication and relationship with the child. The two parents are just as important and valuable and do not belong here to winners and losers. However, because, even in this sensitive and essential piece of legislation, deliberate misinterpretation of provisions and micro-policy go a long way before we say what this draft law regulates, I think it is worthwhile to say what it does not regulate. This draft law, therefore, does not regulate parental differences, does not address both the requirements or objections of a divorce, nor does it deal with nutritional issues, does not impose on parents the treatment of custody, nor does it remove their right to decide on the best management scenario themselves and, of course, does not remove the right to appeal to a court on grounds that each parent can judge sufficient. There is, therefore, no existing right to be waived. On the other hand, provisions are laid down to strengthen them. We harmonise our legislation both with modern reality and with the spirit of international conventions, but also with the good practices of all the advanced countries of the world and our partners in the European Union. The child’s interest, as all modern studies say, is his communication and upbringing by both parents, who love it even when they have decided to live separately. After the separation both parents have reason and role, rights and obligations. But that was practically always the case. The bill introduces this, the joint exercise of parental responsibility by both parents, who have a duty and a right to custody of the child, supervision, education, upbringing, as well as management of his property and representation in each case. For all the crucial issues about his life and future, both parents have a reason. It also introduces mediation as a means of resolving disputes instead of the psycho-corruption of a judicial path, since parents cannot regulate their own issues. The ultimate objective of this initiative is to stop cohabitation as smooth and calm as possible for the benefit of the child. There is a minimum basis for the child’s time of communication with the parent with whom it does not reside in order to ensure adequate communication but also quality, substantial child’s time with both parents. Finally, the bill helps the work of justice by introducing objective criteria for the misuse of parental care. It is of course always in the judge's judge's judgment to take into account other criteria for deciding, but their list sets out a clear basis for reference and removes any margin of misinterpretation. In addition, training programs are being prepared at the National School of Judges so that the judges are fully familiar with the new framework and assimilate the new philosophy that this promotes. Ladies and gentlemen, today we are voting on a bill which comes into our hands after exhausting consultation, with an unprecedented number of comments and interventions by individuals and bodies, of which ultimately has the consent of a vast part of Greek society. The Government of Kyriakos Mitsotakis and the Ministry's leadership handle a sensitive and thorny issue with its determination, attention and value, reforming an institutional framework that is beyond reality itself. We are giving a way out on a major issue that is agitating our society and, in particular, the thousands of children who need to suffer the trivialisation of a troublesome divorce of their parents and many women who otherwise had imagined their lives and have otherwise been brought to them by fate. When there are in the middle of children there is no room to choose camp, there is only room for love, care and understanding about their proper upbringing. As a Member of Parliament, as a woman, I invite you to vote in favour of this bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-05-19-S1-commons.u211	Thank you, Madam President. Mr Ministers, ladies and gentlemen, the day of memory today, a hundred and two years from the Genocide of the Greeks of the Pontou. Maintaining historical memory is our duty. The struggle for the international recognition of Genocide continues. Forty-eight years have passed since the ‘83 that the Urban Code had last reformed in the family relations. In this time it has changed significantly both the active role of parents who play in the lives of their children and the way we approach child rearing as a society. The concept of empathy is now dominated and on this basis we seek to raise children with emphasis on their emotional balance, so that they develop into adults who will be interested in their fellow man. The bill therefore has the interest of the child alone, as is required by the United Nations Convention on the Rights of the Child. Intermediation has not yet gained ground in Greece against the judiciary. The Greeks show confidence - and rightly so - in justice. But the effort made in today's bill is to consolidate mediation as a quicker, more flexible and less costly process, from which both sides are won, especially children. The bill clearly stipulates that parental care is exercised jointly and equally by both parents after the marriage has been settled or the cohabitation agreement has been cancelled. The arrangement extends to children who were born out of marriage when they are recognized by their father. Parental care is a broad concept. It shall include the custody of the person of the child, the administration of his or her property and his or her representation in any case, act or trial. Of course, there is still the possibility that only one parent will exercise custody of the child if both sides decide it or the court decides it to be the best solution for the child. Insert for the first time a fighting presumption in relation to the child's minimum communication time, set out in 1/3 of the total time. In the vast majority of judicial decisions this is happening anyway, i.e. on the basis of the case-law, or about this is the time for communication, which is usually prejudicial to the parent with whom the child does not stay firmly. Scientific research demonstrates how important a child’s mentality plays its contact and communication with both parents. The hearing of the committees was thoroughly analysed by specialist psychologists that children benefit greatly when they spend at least 35% of their time with each parent. In order to avoid any misinterpretation the presumption is combatable, i.e. it is solely up to the judge of the natural court to determine the time of communication, who may be less than 1/3 or more. It shall not be bound or substituted for its key role. Family relationships can't get into a mold. They must also be judged on a case-by-case basis, as defined by the European Commission and the international texts. Today's bill fully respects this need, because every child is unique and every family relationship is different. For the first time objective criteria, which make the exercise of parental care bad, are described as indicative and not restrictive cases, such as the responsible non-compliance with judicial decisions, the blockage of communication between the child and the parent who does not usually live, but also vice versa, the parent's indifference to communicate with his child, the unjustified refusal of the parent to pay a diet, the final judicial decision condemning the parent for domestic violence or crimes against sexual freedom. The provision is a guideline for judges and at the same time a list that is deterrent to parents. Again, cases are indicative. It is for the judge to judge whether, for example, someone is inappropriate to communicate with his child because he has been prosecuted against him but has not yet been convicted of domestic violence. Finally, one of the most important innovations in the bill is the provision for court training programs within the National School of Judges. In some respects, there is a consistent view that family courts should be founded in Greece. In fact, the continuing training of judges and prosecutors is important. In fact, the current provisions stipulate that judges who are judging cases relating to parental/child relations have previously participated in specific training courses in order to foster the spirit of the new provisions. Overall, the public debate on the role of both parents following the divorce and the time that each parent must have with his child has triggered disagreements and constructive proposals. Moreover, the great resonance of the draft law in society also emerges from a large participation in the consultation process with about fifteen thousand comments. Today's bill does not change the legislative framework in the interests of one or the other, since this is not the role of the legislator. It highlights the protection of children and adapts the legal framework to the modern needs of society. This is a courageous reform, Minister. The new provisions reflect the undeniable need for all of us to change attitudes: society, parents, judges. It will take time until we realize that it is not for the benefit of the child that it is alienating it from a parent or for the refusal to pay food or for constant legal disputes on any simple matter. We have a duty as a society to take care of our children. Besides, children always reflect the kind of care they take. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-05-19-S1-commons.u269	Thank you, Mr President. Mr Ministers, ladies and gentlemen, divorce in Greece once was a marginal phenomenon in the life of couples. By 1984 we had five divorces per ten thousand inhabitants. In 1992 eight divorces the same proportion of population, while in 2018 we reached eighteen divorces per ten thousand inhabitants. Obviously there is a marked growth trend in divorces in the years 2008-2018, which exceeds 45%. The vast majority of divorced couples have children. Undoubtedly, the structures of Greek society, couples' relations and the relationship between parents and children have changed greatly in the forty years since the creation of the current family law. The roles of the two sexes have changed, the participation of the two parents, the mother and the father, in child rearing has been differentiated. For many years society, including experts, have underestimated the role of father in the family and development of the child, considering that his impact is less important than that of the mother. But today a great deal of scientific research suggests that the father, through his interaction with the child and through his involvement in life, plays a unique and crucial role in his mental health and personality at all stages of development. After divorce children also need the time and occupation of both their parents and there is neither empirical nor scientific basis for exclusive parental care. Scientific studies, as well as the experience of everyday life teach us that children, who have both parents in their lives after divorce, have better quality of life, have better developmental results, improved academic performance, improved indicators of psychological, emotional and social well-being, while reducing the incidence of problematic behaviours. So there is a clear correlation between the presence of the two parents and the mental state and children's development performance. Showing sensitivity and social reflexes, the Ministry of Justice comes to address these new social data and incorporate our legislation. Assisting him anyway a childcentre of family law, he introduces innovations that meet the needs of the modern family and, above all, the needs of the children. Joint exercise of parental responsibility. There is no scientific research to deny that the child is benefiting from the presence of both parents in his life. This regulation highlights the uniqueness of the role each parent performs, while at the same time reducing the disputes as divorced people know in advance that they will be invited to decide together for the life of their child. For the first time, the presumption of minimum communication time is introduced, ensuring that the parent, who is not staying with the child, will have substantial communication with him and will not be the one-hour visitor in his life. Mediation is being established as a mandatory stage in the resolution of family differences. Intermediation gives parents the opportunity to discover for themselves what model of communication and diligence fits their own separate case before reaching the natural judge and before they end up in dispute. The nature of the mediation process, the compromise climate and the confidentiality that characterize it contribute to improving relations between the two sides and normalising conflicts. Finally, for the first time, criteria for the misuse of parental care are laid down. We provide the judge with an extra tool in order to make it easier to define inappropriate parental behaviour. It is an indicative numbering of unacceptable behaviours that do not restrict the judge who remains the final assessor of the situation. In this direction, training programmes are being organised for judges who will judge family differences cases by qualified psychologists, sociologists and social workers. This reference to examples of malaise in parental care also helps parents, given that it gives them a point of reference to know in advance what behavior they might have led to the loss of custody. Both scientific studies and research and daily practice show that conditions in Greece are mature to change the existing regime. The legislative initiative, which we are debating today, has caused a strong public debate and is, in fact, a response from the state to this debate, a debate which is not only today, but has been evolving for years at the same time as changes in society, structures and standards of the family. Consensus and understanding are necessary in order for divorced parents to develop a level playing field. This framework is therefore created by this legislation, which encourages parents to accept cooperation in bringing up their children, because raising a child is primarily an individual right of children that our state must protect. Thank you very much.
