ParlaMint-GR_2021-05-19-S1-commons.u281	Thank you, Mr President. Mr Ministers, ladies and gentlemen, I cannot help but mention the peculiarity of today's day, the Memorial Day of the Greek Genocide of the Pontus, about a century after this sad event of our fellow Greeks were killed, displaced. It is our duty, therefore, to honor their memory, remember and fight for justice and recognition of this day. I move on to this draft law, which is another reform of the New Democracy Government, a reform of family law in a sector of civil law with a significant social impact. In the consultation that has taken place, and in general the views expressed to date, many problems have been identified, which have emerged in the relations between parents and children following the cessation of cohabitation or the resolution of marriage, problems which I myself have seen as a minister of justice, a lawyer and a legal representative in such cases, which by their very nature have a particular sensitivity, reflecting in the spirit, progress and development of children, but also in the normal social and family life. We have all seen strong disputes, and for a long time and in order to deal with various issues between parents, after the end of cohabitation. In 2016, nine thousand seven hundred ninety cases were brought in. In 2019 we reached the fifteen thousand eight hundred sixty-three cases. This demonstrates the necessary intersection in the reform of family law, thirty-eight years after Law 1329. They are disputes which inevitably go through different ways and degrees in children and have a negative impact on the relationship with parents, and it is certain that we need to focus on trying to resolve these disputes consensually and out-of-courtly, with effective participation in this effort, both by lawyers and judges and also by ombudsmen. In this draft law, therefore, there are two important provisions. The resolution of disputes by mediation by specialised ombudsmen is encouraged so that recourse to the court is the last option. In addition, special training is provided for young judges who will deal with these sensitive cases by the National School of Judges and special training seminars for existing judges. Ladies and gentlemen, we must all agree that in such cases parents must be encouraged to resolve their disputes out of court and mediation where necessary, in order to provide the necessary support to parents so as to avoid disputes. But, in the essence of the disputes that have arisen in such cases, we should focus on the most serious problems. And I shall refer to what I have seen from my experience in handling such cases. The phenomenon of a removal from the parent's child, which is not diligent, is observed because of the fact either the communication time is not sufficient or because the parent does not participate in important decisions concerning the child or disagrees with some of them, which is another serious point of friction with the other parent. But there are two other serious cases that alienate, I could say, one parent from his child, cases that unfortunately happen. The phenomenon of parenting changes the place of his or her child's residence and place of residence at a distant place in relation to the residence of the other parent, which is particularly common in marriages with parents, originating in different regions, but also in marriages between foreigners. Even more serious is the case that a parent, who takes custody and resides with the child, with psychological pressure and war, turns the minor child against the other parent, effectively transferring his own problems with the other parent to the child and using the child to avenge his ex-husband. In these cases the right to communicate with the parent who violates it is violated, fortified behind the child's refusal to communicate with the other parent. Ladies and gentlemen, it is indeed the case that I as a lawyer have been very difficult to deal with, and it is also cases where the judge cannot deal with them enough many times, so far not having the necessary scientific support from qualified psychologists and social workers. Of course, it is very common that the phenomenon of a parent is in breach of the maintenance requirement, as it has been established by the court, which can come from either the intensity of the disputes or even the removal from the child, precisely because of this limited communication. Because I find it hard to understand otherwise a parent’s refusal to contribute to the necessary support for his child. All these are problems that have arisen in recent years and it is certain that there was a need for legislative intervention. On these issues, this draft law provides a solution. A fighting presumption shall be established in one third of the child's communication time with the parent who will not spend any more time with the child in accordance with Article 13, which replaces Article 1520 of the Civil Code. This presumption helps parents co-operate with the care of their children and creates not only conditions for more time, but also for quality communication time with the child, especially by the parent with whom he does not reside. Because, as has been seen on several occasions, the non-communication of the child with the parent and vice versa leads to intense changes in mental health, which are growing during its development. In this draft law, the main objective criteria for misuse of parental responsibility are laid down in Article 14, which replaces Article 1532 of the Civil Code, such as non-payment of maintenance, non-compliance with the judgments and agreements of divorced parents in connection with the communication of the other parent and the burglary relationships of the child with the other parent. The way in which parental care is exercised is thus assessed so that, if necessary, the competent court can redefined the distribution and the special way in which it is exercised. I should also like to make it clear here that each case is always different. The competent judge will have the power to examine the specificities of each case and to differentiate wherever necessary and to make an appropriate decision, in the light of all the interests of the child. Ladies and gentlemen, this draft law is a necessary one for modern society reform, which comes to regulate key issues in relation to father and mother relations for the benefit of children. The role of both parents is strengthened in relation to child rearing. Cooperation between the two parents shall be strengthened and encouraged. The role of the Ombudsman is strengthened and the role and position of the judge in dealing with these sensitive cases is strengthened. This is a bill with purely childcentres. It is in the best interests of the child, and I am sure that, with this reform, many of the problems that so far arise in the implementation of the existing legislative framework will be tackled. I therefore invite you to vote on this draft law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-05-19-S1-commons.u283	Thank you, Mr President. May I too join my voice together with the other colleagues for today, memory day for the Greeks of Pontou. hundreds of thousands of Greeks have been lost, and I think that this day we should honour and give it the importance and value that we need. Mr President, Minister, ladies and gentlemen, we are called upon to discuss and vote on a bill on a big social issue on the next day of a divorce. The changes introduced by this bill concern relations between parents and children in the event of divorce. This reform of family law comes after thirty-eight years and it is now necessary, because in these years the contact between parents and children in the daily life of the family has changed. Both the role of the father and the mother in the family have been differentiated. Both the needs and obligations of children and parents have increased and changed. The foundation of the family has been transformed, but it remains strong for the Greek society. The other was Greece of 1983 and the other was Greece of 2021. So all these requirements of modern times have led to the specific arrangements of the bill. This draft law is a product of long consultation and discussion with all sides and all the relevant bodies, a difficult one, due to the sensitive nature of the object of the bill and at the same time an important initiative, which was brought to fruition by the Ministry of Justice. So what do we do with this draft law? We are establishing a new institutional framework, which will govern the relationship between parents and children after the loss of spouses' cohabitation or divorce. The joint exercise of parental care by both parents is established. The fightable presumption of 1/3 is established for the communication of the child with the parent with whom he does not live. This ensures the balanced communication of the child with both parents. Clear and objective criteria for misuse of parental care, such as domestic violence or non-payment of food, are being established. The judge in cases of domestic violence, or judging the general attitude of the parent, will be able to restrict or even cancel the parent’s communication with the child. There is no tolerance for us in the incidents of domestic violence. Both women and children are protected from such phenomena. It should be noted that all the provisions will be applied and interpreted in accordance with the Council of Europe Convention on the Prevention and Combating of Violence against Women, the Istanbul Convention. Alternative methods of resolving family differences are also introduced, such as mediation. Finally, special programmes of the National Judicial School are being established for the training of judges, who will judge family differences from specialized scientists. Ladies and gentlemen, these arrangements have the focus of the child. We are seeking to create a legal framework so that parents can consult themselves to resolve their disputes out of court, avoid disputes and quarrels between children who are harming each other, as well as both parents having a presence and a reason in the upbringing and progress of their children. It is wrong to talk about 'winners' and'required' parents. Divorce or separate parental cohabitation is a development that can happen in the life of a couple and the state must provide to ensure the child’s normal upbringing. This is undoubtedly a big social issue. It is clear that it will not only be solved by legislative arrangements, as it even goes beyond our role. The aim of the initiative, however, is the presence of both parents in the everyday life and needs of children. Many times when there is a marriage solution or separate cohabitation, one of the two parents disappears from the life of the child and usually becomes the "father of the weekend" or the "father of the weekend", which has unpleasant and negative consequences for the psychology and progress of the children. The role of father and mother in children's lives is different, essential and decisive. One cannot under any circumstances replace the other. So this is what we are seeking to ensure with this bill that children are raised by the presence of both parents. Ladies and gentlemen, this bill has raised many reactions and individual disagreements. It is reasonable and perfectly understandable that this is the case. We are not talking about an economic bill, nor about a bill on labour. It has no ideological, no party character and goes beyond the left and right. It's about our everyday life, the hard core of the family, and sensitive issues. We didn't make a bill in favour of Dads and Moms, neither in favour of moms and parents. It's a bill in favor of children and their right upbringing. That is why both the present and the future of our country are concerned. Because if children are not born, if Greek citizens are not born, we have no future.
